Boy Meets World Cast Reveals Major Salary Differences in Final Season

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Boy Meets World alum Rider Strong revealed how a misunderstanding of his contract led to him receiving a much lower salary than his costar Ben Savage.

Rider Strong was meeting a world of salary differences.

The Boy Meets World alum got candid about the vast pay disparity between himself and costar Ben Savage on the seventh—and final—season of the series in Book Meets World: The Definitive Inside Story of Hit Sitcom Boy Meets World, a book he co-wrote with costars Danielle Fishel and Will Friedle.

As Strong explained, he was uninterested in starring on season seven of the show—which ran from 1993 to 2000—but had “misread” his contract and believed that he was already “locked in” for the season, according to an excerpt from the book shared by Entertainment Weekly. The misunderstanding led to a call from creator Michael Jacobs, who assured him there would be no season eight, only the final, seventh season.

“I was already in college,” Strong wrote, per the outlet, “and I didn’t want to do the show anymore.”

However, 46-year-old recalled that Jacobs pitched him “hard,” telling Strong he could head to college the following year.

“I stopped him, and I said, ‘You realize, they’re talking about adding an eighth season.’ He was like, ‘No, we’re not. We’re talking about adding a seventh,’” Strong wrote, adding that Jacobs allegedly told him, “‘Okay, so we’re just thinking about adding a year that you already thought was there, so we’re good.’”

The meeting left the former child star feeling “confused,” although he knew his manager would be happy that he signed on for another season. Still, he felt alone throughout the negotiation process.

“I thought, why is nobody looking out for me and just saying, ‘Rider, if you don’t want to do this, you don’t have to?’” he said. “There was no sense of that.”

“I had my own relationship with my manager, my own relationship with my agent, which is why I walked into a meeting with Michael by myself and got completely screwed,” he continued, noting that “if he could go back, it would have ended differently.”

And while the Pod Meets World host wishes he hadn’t stayed on for a seventh season, he did notice that costar Savage “held out” on committing to the final season after getting accepted to Stanford University, which Strong said led to Savage receiving a “huge pay increase.”

“There was a lot of miscommunication. If we had actually talked to each other, we probably should have just said, ‘We’re all equal here, let’s go [negotiate together].’ But there was no sense of that,” Strong claimed in the book. “It seemed that Ben getting into Stanford was his biggest negotiating tactic, because the contract ended. They wanted the seventh season, but he was like, ‘Are you going to not allow me to go to college?’ He stuck to his guns, and it worked out.”

As for how much Savage’s pay was increased? According to Strong, he was able to negotiate a salary close to that of costar Bill Daniels—an established actor who was the highest paid actor on the show—earning “close to six figures per episode.”

And as Strong’s costar Friedle noted in the book, he and the rest of the cast “were making nowhere near that,” adding that he regrets not entering negotiations with Strong “so that the two of them were making the same salary.”

E! News has reached out to Savage and Jacobs for comment, but has not heard back.

For a look at what more stars have shared about their own salaries through the years, keep reading.

The Jackass star reflected on the "comical" amount of money he was paid for doing death-defying stunts on the MTV series' first season in 2000.

"When it was all said and done, after taxes, I was paid less than $1,500 for the entire first season of Jackass," the daredevil told Playboy in June 2026. "Before the show came out, my sister kicked me out of the house. I was broke, unemployed, and homeless, and a star on this big MTV show."

Oscar winner Jennifer Lawrence was paid $25 million to star in Adam McKay’s ensemble disaster comedy Don’t Look Up. But her costar and fellow Oscar winner Leonardo DiCaprio got $30 million.

“Look, Leo brings in more box office than I do,” Lawrence told Vanity Fair for its’ December 2020/January 2021 issue. “I’m extremely fortunate and happy with my deal. But in other situations, what I have seen—and I’m sure other women in the workforce have seen as well—is that it’s extremely uncomfortable to inquire about equal pay. And if you do question something that appears unequal, you’re told it’s not gender disparity but they can’t tell you what exactly it is.”

Her sanguine attitude was hard-fought: When the disparity between Lawrence’s compensation and that of her male costars for 2013’s American Hustle was revealed in the 2014 Sony email hack, it prompted an industry-wide conversation about the gender pay gap in Hollywood.

"I'm so fortunate to have my job. My problem is not money," Lawrence told the U.K.’s Channel 4 News in 2017. "I wasn't upset that I only got this many millions for a movie. That's ridiculous. I was angry about the unfairness and inequality."

The *NSYNC alum revealed that he and his fellow bandmates JC Chasez, Joey Fatone, Chris Kirkpatrick and Justin Timberlake didn't make much money during their years with the group.

“We had a per diem: $35 a day,” he revealed on The Zach Sang Show in 2026. “Our first check was a $10,000 check after years of No. 1 albums, sellout tours. I don’t know how many millions they made off of merchandise because merchandise was insane.”

“And then they finally gave us a check for $10,000,” he recalled. “You’re like, ‘Oh, thanks.’ We should be grateful for that, been working for free for two, three years now.”

Less comparable to DiCaprio’s salary: The $60,000 “before commissions and taxes” that Jonah Hill was paid for 2013’s The Wolf of Wall Street, while the titular wolf made $10 million.

“They gave me the lowest amount of money possible,” Hill recalled on The Howard Stern Show in 2014. But it was the chance to appear in a Martin Scorsese movie, so the Moneyball alum wanted to get the deal done before anyone could change their mind.

“I would sell my house and give him all my money to work for [Scorsese],” explained Hill, who earned his second Best Supporting Actor nomination for his turn as a squirrelly substance-abusing stockbroker. “This isn't what you make money for. You do 22 Jump Street or you do other things, and you can pay your rent. I would have done anything in the world. I would do it again in a second. This isn't about money. You should do things that you care about."

According to Dylan Sprouse, his residuals from filming The Suite Life of Zack & Cody with twin brother Cole Sprouse is anything but suite.

"The only residual check I've ever received for The Suite Life at all was a crossover episode with Hannah Montana for five cents," the Disney Channel alum shared during a 2026 episode of his Wildmen podcast. "In a time where residuals would have been great, not a single paycheck for being arguably one of the most popular shows on television."

While there's no cash prize for nabbing Dancing With the Stars' mirrorball trophy, “whoever makes it to the finale, you all get paid up until then," Jenna Johnson, who won season 33 with partner Joey Graziadei, shared. "And you do get a nice bonus.”

"If you win," Johnson, who noted pro contracts are dependent on seniority, continued. "it’s not like you get a million dollars. You’re just getting a cute trophy together.” As for those who say farewell to the ballroom early, well, as she explained, "You’re guaranteed until a certain amount of weeks.” According to multiple reports, dancer salaries start at around $1,200 to $1,600 an episode, which can increase to up to of $100,000 per season. (ABC has not confirmed these reports.) On the flip side, celebrities receive a starting salary of $125,000. As they cha-cha further into the competition, stars  receive additional bonuses with the semifinalists and finalists getting paid up to $50,000 extra.

The actress seemingly confirmed that the Friends cast—including Jennifer Aniston, Courtney Cox,David Schwimmer and Matt LeBlanc—get $20 million in residuals per year from the show re-airing, quipping to The Times in April 2026 that if she had to guess why, it's “Because Phoebe Buffay was so great?"

But in actuality, she praised the talents of her costars for their performances.

“I felt I did OK, but Jennifer and Courteney? Amazing,” she raved. “David and Matt? They had me laughing so hard. And then Matthew [Perry]—he was just beyond us all.”

To hear Joe Giudice tell it, his and ex-wife Teresa Giudice's salary for season one of The Real Housewives of New Jersey was nothing to flip over. "We got paid nothing the first season," he detailed on daughter Gia Giudice's Casual Chaos podcast in August 2025. "I'll even say, it was like $25,000. It was ridiculous. But, whatever."

And the ingredientses they put into that initial fame-making season weren't cheap. "I think the first year we had that big party in my great room," the father of four reflected. "I think that show cost me $150,000 the first year."

Oprah Winfrey happily accepted only a little bit of green to star in 1985’s The Color Purple.

“They were only offering $35,000 to be in this film, and it is the best $35,000 I ever earned,” the billionaire media mogul told Essence in 2023. “It changed everything and taught me so much.”

The actress revealed that she made about $20,000 per episode of Two and a Half Men with a 5 percent raise each season, making about $43,000 for one episode in the final season.

When she scored the role of late Tejana singer Selena Quintanilla in the 1997 biopic Selena, Jennifer Lopez became the first Latina actress to earn $1 million for a film.

“I was too young and didn’t know what the hell was going on,” Lopez told Variety in 2019, reflecting on her breakout movie. “It was great they offered me a million dollars. I feel like everyone was making a statement.”

And it paved the way for her to make hundreds of millions more, though sometimes her participation doesn’t cost a thing.

“I do things because I love them,” Lopez explained to GQ in 2019. “I didn't get paid a whole bunch of money for Hustlers. I did it for free and produced it. I bank on myself, you know? Like Jenny From the Block—I do what I love."

Adrien Brody didn’t do The Brutalist for the money. Rather, he took on the role of a Hungarian architect suffering for his art in post-WWII America for $250,000 and won his second Oscar for Best Actor.

“I need a studio movie now, because I’ve poured it all into this,” Brody quipped to Variety, referring to the “barn that looks like a castle” he’s renovating in upstate New York.

“Do you guys know what they pay us?” Pete Davidson cracked to New York magazine when asked ahead of Saturday Night Live's 50th anniversary special how he spent his money when he was first hired at the age of 20 in 2014. “It’s like three grand an episode, so I think I got dinner.”

Interestingly, the salaries have remained proportional for SNL newcomers, with season one stars John Belushi, Jane Curtin, Gilda Radner, Dan Aykroyd and Chevy Chase reportedly earning $750 an episode back in 1975. Which was roughly equivalent to $3,300 in 2014, according to the Consumer Price Index Inflation calculator. (Though the $750 of 1975 is $4,428 in 2025.)

While Kenan Thompson makes a lot more than $3,000 per episode of SNL now that he’s in his record 22nd season, the former Nickelodeon star had to start somewhere.

“That first commercial when they paid me, it was $800,” the All That alum revealed in Demi Lovato‘s 2024 documentary Child Star. “I was 12 so that may as well have been a million dollars."

It turns out Gilmore Girls’ status as a beloved rewatchable classic is literally priceless.

“There really are no residuals on Netflix,” Lauren Graham, who played Lorelai Gilmore for seven seasons and then reprised the role for 2016’s Gilmore Girls: A Year in the Life, said on Jimmy Kimmel Live in March 2025. “But I’ve been paid in love and appreciation.”

Feeling not so appreciated, Tommy Dorfman got specific about her 13 Reasons Why paycheck during the 2023 SAG-AFTRA strike—which was prompted in part (as was the WGA strike) by streamers like Netflix not making it a practice to pay additional compensation to cast and crew once a show had become a big hit.

"my earnings for the entire first season of 13 reasons why were $29,953.24 prior to agency and manager fees (20%) and taxes," Dorfman wrote on Threads July 24. "8 episodes over six months." She continued, “i did all of the promo and had KEY ART for this show, flew round trip from NYC to SF to shoot for every episode, was kept for days without pay/working. i barely qualified for insurance."

And, Dorfman added, “within the first 28 days of release, the show's season 1 garnered a total of 476 million view hours. this is why we strike."

Without naming names or numbers, Dax Shepard told fellow Parenthood alum Lauren Graham when she appeared on his podcast that he knew he made “among the lowest of all the actors” on their NBC drama, which ran for six seasons.

But, the Armchair Expert host stressed that he did not mind.

“Every job I had ever had in my life prior to Parenthood, I made a point to find out what everyone was making,” he said. “I'd always figure it out—either a conversation where I get it out of them or I backchannel through an agent."

So he made a point to purposely not seek out such info about his Parenthood family, which helped with enjoying the experience even when he did get an inkling of where he was on the pay pyramid.

“I got a dollar for writing the script,” George Clooney told the Los Angeles Times in 2005 about the financial strings he pulled to get his film Good Night, and Good Luck made. “I had to endorse my check for directing and turn in my acting salary. [Actor, cowriter and coproducer Grant Heslov] and I each made a buck for doing it.”

His labor of love about CBS News journalist Edward R. Murrow speaking truth to power in the 1950s had legs, though: Making his Broadway debut, Clooney wrapped his months-long stint playing Murrow in a stage adaptation of the film  in June 2025. And with the play having grossed a Broadway record (for a nonmusical) $3.3 million during a preview week before it officially opened April 3, he made a lot more than $1.

For his chilling turn as Patrick Bateman in 2000’s American Psycho, Christian Bale was paid “the absolute minimum they were legally allowed to pay me” because no one in the production really wanted to hire him except director Mary Harron, the Welsh actor told GQ in 2022.

He made so little, Bale shared, that he remembered “sitting in the makeup trailer and the makeup artists were laughing at me because I was getting paid less than any of them.”

No, the Game of Thrones cast didn't get paid $300,000 per episode, according to Emilia Clarke.

“We didn’t earn that much," she told Variety in 2026. "Can you imagine? I’d have been driving a couple of Porsches!"

However, the actress—who played Daenerys Targaryen for eight seasons between 2011 and 2019—noted that the money she made throughout the HBO show did provide her with enough financial security to pay off her parents’ mortgage.

His uncle may have been Rico, but Jon Heder confirmed in 2010 that he was “initially” only paid $1,000 for Napoleon Dynamite.

After the irreverent comedy became a sleeper hit, he was able to renegotiate for a piece of the profits, telling the New Zealand Herald, “They went a little bit higher.”

Djimon Hounsou said he’s “definitely underpaid” in Hollywood despite his prestigious list of credits, including Amistad and Blood Diamond.

"I've been in this business and making films now over two decades," the Beninese actor told CNN’s Larry Madowo in January 2025. "And still—with two Oscar nominations and been in many big blockbuster films—and yet I’m still struggling financially to make a living."

Rebel Wilson detailed in her 2024 memoir Rebel Rising that she was paid $3,500 to do Bridesmaids, “a fee that I then had to pay directly to the Screen Actors Guild to join the union. So really, I got paid nothing.”

Still, she wrote, the experience was “everything!”

And the Australian actress’ star rose ever a-ca higher, with her fee going from the SAG minimum scale of $65,000 for 2012’s Pitch Perfect to $10 million for 2017’s Pitch Perfect 3. Wilson wrote that she raked in $20 million total for the threequel, Isn’t It Romantic and The Hustle, though—reality alert—she “lost almost 50 percent to taxes, 10 percent to agents and 5 percent” to her lawyer. But even after those obligations and paying her publicist, business manager and assistant, Wilson “netted what to me was an absolute fortune.”

In late 2017, Ellen Pompeo negotiated a new Grey’s Anatomy contract for $575,000 per episode, a seven-figure signing bonus and two full backend equity points worth an estimated $6 million to $7 million.

“I’m 48 now, so I’ve finally gotten to the place where I’m OK asking for what I deserve, which is something that comes only with age,” Pompeo told The Hollywood Reporter in 2018. “Because I’m not the most ‘relevant’ actress out there. I know that’s the industry perception because I’ve been this character for 14 years. But the truth is, anybody can be good on a show season one and two. Can you be good 14 years later? Now, that’s a f--kin’ skill.”

Reflecting more recently on how she always made less than costar Patrick Dempsey, even though she was the Grey in question, Pompeo said she didn’t begrudge him his money.

But, she said on a March 2025 episode of Call Her Daddy, “Just being that I was the namesake of the show, I deserved the same and that was harder to get. I wasn't salty about him getting what he got. I was salty that they didn't value me as much as they valued him and they never will.”

Jamie Lee Curtis recalled getting paid $2,000 a week—so $8,000 in total—for 1978’s Halloween, her movie debut. “I mean,” she told Rotten Tomatoes in 2018, “nobody got paid, anything.”

Yet while the scream queen earned four figures, director John Carpenter’s friend Nick Castle felt like a king making $25 per day to share the role of Michael Myers. (It’s Anthony Moran whose face you see when the killer’s mask comes off for a freaky second, and editor Tommy Lee Wallace also logged screentime as Michael.)

“That was a lot at the time!” Castle, who attended film school at USC with Carpenter, told Vanity Fair in 2018. “You have to remember: My interest in doing the film was being on set, so I could demystify the experience of filmmaking and directing. I expected to hang around the set for no money. But hey, $25 per day, and all I had to do was wear a rubber mask.”

Taraji P. Henson earned a Best Supporting Actress Oscar nomination for 2008’s The Curious Case of Benjamin Button, but years later she was still fired up by the $150,000 she accepted to be in the movie—after producers rejected the $500,000 quote she brought to the table.

As for the film’s leads Brad Pitt and Cate Blanchett, “You don’t hear a lot of $20 million quotes anymore, but at this time that was happening,” Henson said on the Ladies First With Laura Brown podcast in 2021. “And rightfully so—I’m not saying they shouldn’t have paid Cate and Brad what they deserved.”

But, she continued, “I’m bringing a certain amount to the seat too and I felt like what I was asking at that time in my career was fair, was fair to the ticket sales that I would contribute to this big film. Wouldn’t do it.” Henson was “gutted” when they offered $100,000, she recalled. “When it was all said and done I got $150,000, but I had to swallow my pride, baby.”

Breaking it down, “I know people go, ‘$150,000, that’s a lot of money!’” Henson acknowledged. “I don’t ever want people to think that I’m ungrateful because that is not me.” But, she calculated, “Uncle Sam is going to take 50 percent of that, so now you’re left with, what? $75,000. Now before Uncle Sam takes the money, I have to pay my team before taxes, 30 percent. So once Uncle Sam takes his 75, then I got another 30 that’s coming off of that 75, so I may have made $40,000?”

While Blanchett has made plenty of money over the years, it didn’t all come from playing royal elf Galadriel in The Lord of the Rings franchise, though it grossed $2.9 billion at the worldwide box office.

“Are you kidding me? No,” she retorted on Watch What Happens Live in August 2024 when Andy Cohen guessed LOTR was her biggest paycheck. “No one got paid anything to do that movie…I mean, I basically got free sandwiches, and I got to keep my ears.”

They weren’t paid a precious amount, that is. Orlando Bloom, who played elven warrior Legolas, shared on The Howard Stern Show in 2023 that he made $175,000 for the whole trilogy.

Damian Lillard is back and Ja Morant is here. How exactly is this going to work? | Bill Oram

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

In the history of basketball, we have certainly seen some why-would-they-do-that? pairings.

Jerseys and sleeves. Kawhi Leonard and a tree sponsorship. Utah and "the Jazz."

Once, there was even Detlef Schrempf and a headband, if you can believe that.

We're about to find out if they belong on such a list.

Because of all the things you might have said the Trail Blazers needed as they prepared to welcome Lillard back after making a playoff run without him, a slight-of-stature ball-dominant guard with a questionable jump shot would not have topped anyone's list.

Depth on the wing? Yes. Shooting? Definitely. Defense? Always.

Morant doesn't particularly address any of those, and yet, he's here, a dynamic two-time All-Star in need of a second act, being paid a team-high $42.1 million to play the same position as not just Lillard, but Jrue Holiday and Scoot Henderson.

Heck, the Blazers' most valuable point guard last season was forward Deni Avdija.

"I'm sure there will be questions about fit," General Manager Joe Cronin said Monday.

The Blazers have little more than three weeks before the regular season begins in which to figure that out. At the team's annual media day on Monday, Morant appeared in a fresh white No. 1 Trail Blazers jersey. It's happening.

"It's easy for people to be like, 'Man how is this going to work,'" Lillard offered. "'They can't do this, they can't do that.' But being on the floor with them … we can be a dangerous team and I really believe that."

The onus is on the Trail Blazers starting this month to prove that they bet right in acquiring Morant. And that pairing him with Lillard isn't the boondoggle conventional wisdom suggests it will be.

Lillard noted how many teams that succeeded in the playoffs last year, think San Antonio with its four point guards, relied on numerous playmakers. To his credit, Morant said he would be comfortable coming off the bench, if necessary.

But they would not be the first NBA team to fall into the trap of believing they are capable of adjusting or that they will be comfortable sacrificing. It isn't uncommon to see players in those situations default to familiar habits and sensibilities once the season is underway or struggles come.

That's what made a lot of the positive energy around what sure seems like an awkward roster ring so hollow. We're going to need to see it to believe it.

Morant tried to lay out a vision for how it could look. If he drives to the paint and is double-teamed, someone else is inevitably open. That's how basketball works.

"That allows me to have more space to be the best Ja I can be," Morant said.

The 36-year-old Holiday, who would be a more natural complement next to Lillard or Morant than they are to each other, made his own case for why the point guard rotation makes sense.

"It's about being a complete basketball player for myself," he said, "and I think we have a lot of guys like that."

There is a long history of teams talking themselves into superstar additions that make little sense. The Los Angeles Lakers once made a conscious decision to acquire Russell Westbrook and pair him with LeBron James. Nobody made them do that!

The talent and star power of a Lillard-Morant backcourt is undeniable. Still, I remain unconvinced that Cronin, with a very hands-on new owner in his ear, made the deal with winning basketball games at the front of mind.

Although, admittedly, Cronin's biggest wins as a GM have been in the trade market.

The haul of assets he collected for Lillard, only to get him back two years later, will go down as an all-time great swindle. So, too, will the oft-questioned Avdija trade a year later.

Maybe he sees something in a Lillard-Morant pairing that many of us can't.

Morant could return to the All-Star form he had achieved before injuries and off-court issues derailed his ascent. And if he does, the Blazers can either trade him for pieces that fit better or clear out the logjam and build around him.

But to sell the current roster as a basketball strategy doesn't quite pass the smell test.

Neither Lillard nor Morant has achieved stardom due to his defensive acumen. Each player needs the ball in his hands to thrive. If the duo is on the floor together, Avdija is no longer the secondary playmaker, he becomes tertiary.

So, you are left to wonder whether the Blazers sold themselves on Morant because of how he can help the current collection of talent or if they simply saw the opportunity to land a 27-year-old All-NBA talent for cheap, regardless of what it meant for the existing roster construction.

And if you're being honest, you probably don't need to wonder which of the two it is.

"We were very focused and we'll continue to be on … adding to our talent base as much as we can," Cronin said.

That thinking right there may tell you just how far ahead of everyone else the San Antonio Spurs and Oklahoma City Thunder are in the Western Conference. They are positioned to dominate for the next half-decade, if not longer. The only chance any other team has of disrupting that is taking bold risks.

But I thought the goal was to go all in on the twilight years of Lillard's career?

"Dame literally just wants to win," said first-year head coach Micah Nori, whose charge is to sort out arguably the most talented glut of point guard talent in NBA history. "I think that's the only thing left he really has to (do). He's already proven he's a winner, but at the highest level."

Does adding Morant move Lillard closer to a title? Or simply give Portland a potential off-ramp if Lillard is no longer the same player after tearing his Achilles tendon?

Morant was without question a low-risk, high-reward addition. He came at the reasonable price of Jerami Grant and Kris Murray. If it becomes clear that Morant is one point guard too many, or that he's just not the player he once was, some will argue that it didn't cost the Blazers much to try.

However, there is an opportunity cost to messing with Lillard's swan song. If you don't get it right, you risk wasting the gift of his voluntary return last summer. And, not for nothing, Lillard has the option to become a free agent again after this season.

The franchise icon certainly hasn't said anything that would suggest he is unhappy about Morant's presence, although he stopped short on Monday of a full-throated endorsement. He said he knew about the trade before it went through.

When I asked if he thought it was a good idea right away or if he needed time to get on board with the plan, Lillard said, "My position on it was, I'm prepared for whatever."

It's good that he's already thinking about other positions. Because his old one is getting awfully crowded.

ChainLink (LINK), Cardano (ADA), Dogecoin (DOGE) and Ethereum (ETH) Price Analysis for November 11: Bearish Market Hits Critical Fear Levels

📅 2026-11-11  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Chainlink sees a death cross approaching on the chart, with a solid possibility of an aggravation of the current state of affairs on the market. LINK is trading at around $10.49 at the moment, moving below every moving average on the daily chart.

The asset has already lost roughly a third of its value since the late September peak near $15.70, when the rally essentially ran out of steam.

The 50 EMA is rapidly diving down and most likely to cross the 100 EMA sooner rather than later. The 50 EMA sits near $11.70 right now, while the 100 EMA is around $11.40, so the gap between them is getting thin.

EMAs are also converging – an important sign that might cause some worrying certainly. The longest moving average on the chart, at around $11, was lost in early November, and LINK hasn't been able to get back above it since.

Luckily, volume is descending, so there's a good chance that things will stabilize rather than degrade from here. Daily volume in November is visibly lower than during the sell-off at the end of October, which is a good sign.

RSI is moving sideways, in a tunnel around 50, so the situation is, in some sense, neutral for the asset. The indicator hasn't made any sharp moves since mid-October, and the market isn't anywhere near the oversold zone.

Fundamentally speaking, Link also had a strong catalyst: Charles Schwab announced it will add LINK to its crypto trading platform, on par with Solana and Avalanche. The news came out on August 27, but LINK is now trading lower than it was on that day. We should also keep in mind that Schwab hasn't named a launch date yet, so the catalyst might still play out once trading actually goes live.

For now, the closest support is the $10 price threshold, where buyers already stepped in twice over the last two weeks. If the death cross gets confirmed and $10 breaks, the drop most likely won't stop there, unfortunately.

Cardano continues the prolonged downtrend, with a possibility of plunging even lower in the foreseeable future. ADA is trading at around $0.1776 at the moment, and it has already lost roughly 37% since the early October peak near $0.28. This week, the price dipped to around $0.1715, back to the levels last seen in mid-August, right before the late-summer rally.

Trend is strong, it's descending, the bear market is certainly having an upper hand here.

RSI is at the bottom though, so there isn't much room for bears to operate here. The indicator is moving below 40, close to the oversold zone, which might slow the selling down for a while.

A strong uptick in volume here would be beneficial. Daily volume in November is among the lowest on the chart, so buyers aren't really showing up even at these prices.

The lack of catalyst is the larger problem: ADA has no traction around it and there's certainly a chance that it won't appear in the foreseeable future.

Fundamentally, ADA had no breakthroughs recently apart from the foundation's support of Cosmos IBC transition to Linux Foundation governance. It's more of an interoperability and institutional infrastructure related development.

If the selling continues, the July range around $0.16 is the next area where ADA could find support. Until volume returns, bracing yourself for more downside might be the wise choice, unfortunately.

Ethereum is certainly struggling, with a solid risk of losing $2,000 price level much sooner than anticipated. Price is already spilling, and somewhat in the freefall. ETH is trading at around $2,095 at the moment, after a big red candle on November 8 took the price down from the $2,230 area.

The asset has already lost roughly 25% since the September peak near $2,800. The current price is also the lowest close since the August breakout, when ETH jumped from the $1,900 area in a matter of days. Most of the late-summer gains are essentially gone.

ETH moves below all major moving averages after losing the black one at around $2,300 in late October. That level is most likely acting as resistance now. The short-term green average is aiming downwards and has already slipped below it, hinting at the aggravation of the current trend.

The November 4 bounce towards $2,450 didn't last either. ETH lost the whole move the very next day, which is an unfortunate sign for anyone hoping for a quick recovery.

RSI is still at the bottom and there's a good chance a bounce is still possible. The asset is clearly oversold. The indicator is hovering close to 30, so sellers might run out of steam soon. If the bounce happens, the black moving average around $2,300 should be the first target.

Volume is descending and overall trend is losing power. Even the November 8 drop came with lower volume than the sell-off at the end of October, so the freefall isn't backed by panic just yet. A strong uptick in selling volume would change the picture, though.

Realistically, the $2,000 price threshold is the first thing buyers have to defend. If it breaks, the next area to watch is around $1,900, where ETH spent most of July and August. A proper recovery, on the other hand, would only start after ETH gets back above $2,230, and that's not happening yet, unfortunately.

Dogecoin falls flat on the strong support level at approximately $0.07 with a possibility of a further recovery. DOGE is trading at around $0.0694 at the moment, just a little below the round number. The price briefly dipped to around $0.0672 this week, but the daily candle closed back near the support.

Support coincides with the point of the previous buying volume surge, where things should become way better if possible. DOGE spent most of July and August around this level before the late-August breakout pushed the price above $0.09 in a matter of days. That breakout came with one of the biggest volume spikes on the chart, so buyers clearly remember the $0.07 area.

Despite, luckily, descending volume, the bearish trend is holding up way too good, with continuous pressure provided on the shoulders of Dogecoin. DOGE moves below all major moving averages, and all of them are aiming downwards. The closest one sits at around $0.0765, most likely acting as the first resistance in case of a bounce.

RSI is closer to the 'oversold' zone and market's reaction to it isn't yet clear. The indicator is moving around 35 right now. Combination of all of those factors suggests rather calmer price movement, than a severe downtrend that can damage Dogecoin's recovery pathing

Apart from the market picture, Dogecoin saw a testnet infrastructure update, with the improvement to L2Reth testnet infrastructure, providing more reliable node restarts, snapshot restorations and deployment configs.

Such updates are essentially technical, so they most likely won't move the price on their own. Still, it's a good sign that development around Dogecoin keeps going even when the market isn't favoring the asset.

The PlayStation Plus app on PC is going away. Don’t have any clue what that was? You’re not alone. Sony’s announcement that it was killing off the cloud-streaming feature was met with a mix of surprise and confusion. “Brother, nobody knew the PS PC App existedâ¦” one player wrote in the PlayStation Blog comments section.

Lots of PS3 games in particular were never ported to PC or modern consoles. I don’t know who exactly was in the market to pay $160 a year to stream Resistance: Fall of Man to their PC, but that option will no longer be available to anyone starting December 10. “I have used the PS Plus app on PC,” another player wrote on the PlayStation Blog. “The primary reason that it did not become a regular thing for me, though, is the requirement for a wired controller. Add in that it was generally janky, and thereâs the problem. It isnât a matter of demand. It has low usage due to functionality, not demand. And there is absolutely demand for PS Plus streaming on Android devices.”

It’s certainly not hard to imagine Sony figuring out a way to boost engagement with game streaming on PC, but it’s been slowly retreating from the platform in recent years. Major first-party games like Ghost of Yotei and Marvel’s Wolverine aren’t slated to get PC ports, and even accessories like the Pulse Elevate wireless speakers have been indefinitely delayed. While that is reportedly down to a manufacturing issue, it doesn’t seem like Sony is eager to get the problem rectified.

Apple TV’s Ambitious Heist Thriller Officially Premieres This Week

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Lade Omotade is a News and Features Author at Collider, covering everything from casting announcements and streaming trends to behind-the-scenes stories and the cultural impact of today's biggest film and TV franchises. Since joining the team in 2024, she has written more than 1,000 news stories, with her work also cited by outlets like AOL and IMDb.

Television is about to get busy this week, with anticipated premieres and returns from some of the most beloved franchises. The week kicks off with the British cozy crime drama The Madame Blanc Mysteries returning for Season 5 on Acorn TV in the US, followed by Netflix's eight-episode binge The Body; a story about a dance-team initiation gone wrong—on a packed Wednesday. Also arriving that day is Season 3 of The Lord of the Rings: The Rings of Power, which finally returns to Prime Video with a four-episode premiere. Rivals also returns Wednesday with the first three episodes of Season 2, Part 2 on Hulu and Disney+.

Later in the week, Blue Lights, BritBox's replacement for The Rookie, returns for Season 4. At the same time, Peacock premieres The Good Daughter, a six-episode mystery thriller adapted from Karin Slaughter's novel and starring Meghann Fahy and Rose Byrne. The weekend then kicks off with a two-episode premiere of Apple TV's ambitious heist series, created by Jim Keeble and Dudi Appleton and starring Anthony Mackie and Jamie Dornan.

Titled 12 12 12, the series is set to premiere this week, on November 13, 2026, with the episodes "All for a F**king Betamax, Dad" and "I Want My Halo Back." It will conclude after eight episodes on December 25. In the thriller, Mackie plays Jack Strickland, a disgraced FBI agent caught in a zero-sum cat-and-mouse game across Europe with Dornan's Paul Galvin, an American career criminal. At the center is a daring, epic raid on a bank vault buried deep beneath Zurich's streets. Each episode explores three timelines of the heist: the 12 months of planning, the 12 hours of the heist, and the 12 days that follow.

Four criminal masterminds. One of them was always going to be yours. Here is who comes for you, why they picked you out of everyone available, and what they want from the encounter.

Your nemesis does not want to beat you. He wants to know you completely, and he is patient enough to wait for it. The frightening part is how good the conversation is.

Your nemesis is not after money, territory or revenge. He is after a demonstration, and you are the person he has chosen to prove his point on.

Your nemesis respects you, which sounds flattering until you work out what it means. He chose you because nobody else was worth his time.

Your nemesis has no personal interest in you whatsoever, and that is precisely what makes him unsurvivable. You are not a rival. You are a stop on the way.

Two years ago, Apple acquired 12 12 12 for release on its streamer, Apple TV. The crime drama then entered production in 2025, with principal photography beginning in May and filming taking place in Budapest and Sopron, Hungary, as well as Austin, Texas. Later, in August, Paramount Television Studios took over production of 12 12 12 following the highly controversial merger of Skydance Media, parent company of Skydance Television, with Paramount Global to form Paramount Skydance.

In addition to creating 12 12 12, Keeble and Appleton wrote and executive produced the series. Kari Skogland directed the pilot and also serves as an executive producer. Additionally, series stars Mackie and Dornan serve as executive producers. Produced by Anonymous Content and Skydance Television, the 12 12 12 cast also includes Jack Kesy, Kali Reis, Ólafur Darri Ólafsson, Agathe Rousselle, and Sallie Harmsen, all of whom were announced in early 2025.

Apple TV's 12 12 12 premieres on Friday, November 13. Stay tuned at Collider for more.

Alexander Skarsgard And Olivia Colman's Romantic Fantasy Movie Is A Sleeper Box Office Hit

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

A sleeper box office hit has emerged. The movie in question is the romantic fantasy flick "Wicker," which stars Alexander Skarsgård ("Godzilla vs. Kong") and Olivia Colman ("The Favourite"), who was part of one of the most shocking Oscar upsets of all time, we might add. Now, she's leading one of the fall season's sneakiest hits.

Directed by Alex Huston Fischer and Eleanor Wilson, "Wicker" opened in theaters on the weekend of October 23, pulling in a solid $5.7 million domestically on a reported $10 million budget. That's not bad, but it debuted at number five on the charts, and it's easy for movies like this to slip by pretty quickly. However, it's managed to hold very well, even as "Godzilla Minus Zero" ruled the box office this past weekend.

As of this writing, "Wicker" has earned $20 million domestically to go with $9 million internationally for a $29 million running total. It hasn't dropped more than 24% on a single weekend, either, meaning that it still has a lot of gas in the tank. Thus, on top of already being in a good position thanks to its reasonable budget, it's on track to profit theatrically before VOD and streaming are even factored in.

Sad to say, that's not the case all too often for smaller, non-franchise movies these days. The film's backer Black Bear has also had a rough start as a distributor in Hollywood, with its Sydney Sweeney vehicle "Christy" flopping at the box office in high-profile fashion last year. This is the hit Black Bear needed.

"Wicker," as it were, centers on an ostracized fisherwoman (Colman) who commissions the local basketmaker (Peter Dinklage) to weave her a husband (Skarsgård). But when this creation proves unexpectedly extraordinary, jealousy and upheaval threatens their quiet village.

It's good news for the movie business in general when a new distributor can emerge on the scene and find success — especially with Warner Bros. and Paramount having recently merged under one roof. Add in Disney having already absorbed Fox in 2019, and Hollywood continues to shrink. So, as much as Black Bear has struggled in the past, it's starting to figure things out.

Indeed, it's been tough sledding for the studio up to this point. Recall that Henry Cavill and Jake Gyllenhaal's action movie "In the Grey" bombed at the box office earlier this year after Lionsgate dumped it and Black Bear picked it up. Matthew McConaughey's "The Rivals of Amziah King" didn't pan out for the studio, either. But if it can emulate the success it's finding with "Wicker" (which is easier said than done, admittedly), there's reason to be optimistic.

Broadly speaking, both theaters and the Hollywood system could use more sleeper hits like this. 2026 has had a lot of bright spots at the box office, among them "Obsession" and "Project Hail Mary," but having smaller movies that can thrive is key to a healthy marketplace. Brad Pitt's survival thriller "Heart of the Beast" also became a sleeper hit while you weren't looking, but that was an expensive movie with an A-list lead. It's not quite the same thing.

Instead, "Wicker" feels closer to Disney turning "Barbarian" into a sleeper hit in 2022. That, in turn, paved the way for director Zach Cregger to make hits like "Weapons" and "Resident Evil." So, yes, we could absolutely use more movies like "Wicker." At the right place and with the right premise, more original, non-franchise ideas can still thrive.

STAT+: Democrats take new step toward investigating Trump’s drug pricing deals

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Test your knowledge with our new weekday mini crossword

John writes about the politics of health care, and he is the author of the twice-weekly D.C. Diagnosis newsletter. You can reach John on Signal at John_Wilkerson.07.

WASHINGTON — The incoming Democratic chairs of key committees have taken a new step toward investigating the most-favored nation drug pricing agreements between drugmakers and the Trump administration.

The drug pricing deals are expected to be a top health care oversight priority for Democrats once they take the majority in January. Earlier this year, the top Democrats on the Senate Finance Committee and the House Energy and Commerce, Ways and Means, and Education and Labor committees requested that companies disclose the details of the deals they struck to tie Medicaid drug prices to levels in peer countries.

Since then, additional companies have signed agreements with the Trump administration. A new round of letters were recently sent to all 27 companies, according to a congressional source familiar with the letters. Endpoints News first reported the new requests.

NVIDIA shareholders are suing the company and CEO Jensen Huang

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The company’s shares have plummeted in response to the exec’s affair with an alleged Chinese spy.

The unfolding drama around NVIDIA CEO Jensen Huang's affair with a woman now accused of being a spy for the Chinese government may be getting its first courtroom chapter. A shareholder in the computing giant has sued accusing NVIDIA and Huang of misleading investors about the nature of the CEO's relationship with Alice Huang, a former executive at the Taiwanese company Megaspeed who is now suspected of being an espionage asset for China. Huang is a common surname; The pair are not related.

Mark Kistenmacher filed the complaint and is seeking class action status for anyone who purchased NVIDIA shares between October 9 and November 8. The first date is when The New York Times published a story on NVIDIA and Megaspeed detailing a connection between the pair, which was rebuffed by NVIDIA as a routine business relationship; the second date is when the NVIDIA founder admitted to an affair and the company publicly disclosed that it was launching its own investigation into whether its top exec had been targeted by a foreign intelligence operation.

In the interim, The New York Times published an in-depth feature on October 29 claiming that the NVIDIA CEO had become the subject of a suspected Chinese intelligence action. The piece cites NVIDIA representative John Rizzo, who said, "Jensen's interactions with Ms. Huang are not unusual or unexpected. Meetings with customers are part of a CEO's job; Jensen talks with thousands of customer executives every year." This statement, the lawsuit alleges, was not true.

The company's shares have dropped in the fallout of the allegations, with NVIDIA on track to lose more than $470 billion in market value over the past two days. "As a result of Defendants' wrongful acts and omissions, and the precipitous decline in the market value of the Company's securities, Plaintiff and other Class members have suffered significant losses and damages," the complaint states.

You may just need to read about this if the examples given here aren't convincing. Maybe one last analogy from me:

It was alway your own money from the start. You own the businesses in the fund and own their assets. You therefore pay yourself with your own money when they make a distribution. Bigger distributions just means shrinking the size of your business investment. If you immediately reinvest, then it doesn't matter (assuming no taxation) and the reinvested money can grow. The alternative is to not pay yourself in the first place, and leave the money invested where it will similarly grow.

Personal Finance (Not Investing) • Re: Risks with autopay? Best practices?

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

If the amount of the autopay is going to be variable (e.g., credit card, gas, electricity and water bills), I don't use autopay because I've had situations where the vendor makes a big mistake. For example, my gas bill was once $6,000 because they changed my gas meter and marked down the wrong starting numbers from the new meter. I pay these bills manually each month, usually via "pull" from the checking account. This gives me a reason to log in to these vendors regularly to keep an eye on things.
I check all bills for accuracy when they arrive (i.e. notified via email). The bill arrives 2-3 weeks before the payment occurs, which is plenty of time to cancel the autopay that month if there is an error. If the bill is accurate, then there's nothing more to do.
I was going to say exactly that. All my utility companies send me emails (I cancelled all paper billing) with the amount they will pull in a couple of weeks. If the amount is the usual, I just let it process. No need to intervene.

Galisteo Media Arts Sets Karl Marx Documentary ‘American Boogeyman’ Based on Book by Andrew Hartman (EXCLUSIVE)

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Galisteo Media Arts has acquired adaptation rights to the 2025 book “Karl Marx in America” by historian Andrew Hartman, and is already developing a doc based on the title: “American Boogeyman: Karl Marx in America.”

According to an official description, “‘American Boogeyman: Karl Marx in America’ will explore the enduring influence of one of America’s most debated — and least understood — intellectual figures, and the provocative contradiction: Marx’s name has long been vilified in American public life, while his ideas have helped shape the nation’s political, economic and cultural debates.” Hartman will serve as an adviser on the project and a key interview subject. Related Stories 'Nobody Wants This' Season 3 Opens With 6.2 Million Views in Four Days Italian Content Investment Dropped 7.4% in 2025 Largely Due to Uncertainty Over Domestic Tax Credits, Rome MIA Market Report Says

“Karl Marx is the ‘American Boogeyman,’ a shadowy figure we have effectively written out of American history, but his ideas have been woven into the fabric of American society,” said Galisteo CEO and producer Robert Rosenheck. “The eight-hour workday, federal income tax, Social Security, public schools and women’s right to vote are all results of Marx’s influence on America. At this pivotal moment in our history it’s time for us to revisit Marx to understand his philosophy and his goals. People may be surprised to learn how integral they are to the American project.”

Rosenheck and Cindy Capobianco founded the Galisteo Group in 2020 with the goal of backing projects with social and environmental impact. Recent films from the company include Tom McCarthy’s historical drama “A Statement” and Shaun Peterson’s 2024 doc “Lover of Men: The Untold History of Abraham Lincoln.”

Galisteo Media Arts is represented by Howie Sanders of Anonymous Content and Alla Savranskaia of Greenberg Glusker LLP.

Mahershala Ali’s New Near-Perfect Action Thriller Officially Removed From 800 Theaters

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

He has covered everything from Marvel to the Oscars, and Marvel at the Oscars. He also writes obsessively about the box office, charting the many hits and misses that are released weekly, and how their commercial performance shapes public perception. In his time at Collider, he has also helped drive diversity by writing stories about the multiple Indian film industries, with a goal of introducing audiences to a whole new world of cinema.

Two-time Oscar winner Mahershala Ali could barely contain his disappointment at Marvel Studios while promoting his new movie, during which he was inevitably asked about the unproduced Blade film that he was attached to. Ali spent several years working on the Blade reboot, while the project saw a revolving door of filmmakers come and go and kept getting pushed back. Marvel boss Kevin Feige eventually admitted that the film was canceled, and that he regrets not being able to get it over the finish line. Ali, on the other hand, said that Marvel could have made the movie had they really wanted to.

He also revealed that he dedicated his life to the project, while putting his career on hold. He declined other roles and moved his family to Atlanta, where the Blade movie was supposed to be shot. The one silver lining of the entire experience, he said slightly sarcastically, was that he was able to learn swordfighting and martial arts on Marvel's dime. These skills eventually came in handy on his latest film, which opened to excellent reviews in theaters.

Despite the near-unanimous praise, the film underperformed at the box office after never quite getting a wide release nationwide. The movie is now available to rent and purchase on the PVOD market, where it'll likely find a larger audience. The movie was directed by Bassam Tariq, who was attached to Ali's Blade project at one point.

We're talking, of course, about Your Mother Your Mother Your Mother, in which Ali plays a religious assassin. The movie now holds a "Certified Fresh" 96% critics' score and a "Verified Hot" 92% audience score on the aggregator website Rotten Tomatoes, where the consensus reads, "Delivering unabashedly pulpy thrills without diminishing its big heart, Your Mother Your Mother Your Mother is an inventive and tonally unique action flick where Mahershala Ali's soulful performance cuts deepest of all."

Distributed domestically by Amazon, which saw success this year with Project Hail Mary, The Sheep Detectives, and Verity, the movie opened in six theaters before expanding to 16 and then 34. At its widest, Your Mother Your Mother Your Mother was playing in 824 theaters nationwide. It ultimately grossed $3.6 million at the domestic box office and was made available on the PVOD market on November 3. According to FlixPatrol, Your Mother Your Mother Your Mother is currently among the most popular movies on the domestic iTunes chart. Stay tuned to Collider for more updates.

MLB rumors: Is anybody worried about Tarik Skubal’s medicals with possible $400 million contract on way?

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

MLB rumors: Is anybody worried about Tarik Skubal's medicals with possible $400 million contract on way? appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

As rumors surround star pitcher Tarik Skubal and it's expected that whoever signs him will give him a contract worth around $400 million, there could be concerns regarding his health and medicals. While the contract offer for Skubal is yet to come, this MLB insider gives insight into how the league is looking at Skubal and his health heading into the offseason.

Potential health concerns come from last season, when he had to have surgery on his elbow to remove loose bodies, but he returned after four weeks. Before that, the only other notable operations for Skubal happened in 2022 when he needed flexor tendon surgery, and also in 2019 when he underwent Tommy John Surgery.

According to Will Sammon, Ken Rosenthal, and Katie Woo of The Athletic, the lead people of teams interested in Skubal "want a better understanding of what to expect" regarding health, especially since the pitcher is turning 30 years old on Nov. 20.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

"As executives gathered for the general managers' meetings on Monday, teams holding interest in Tarik Skubal, the market's far-and-away best free agent, were assessing his medical reports," Sammon, Rosenthal, and Woo wrote.

Despite the aforementioned operations, that hasn't stopped Skubal from being one of the top pitchers in baseball, winning two AL Cy Young Awards when with the Detroit Tigers. After being traded to the Los Angeles Dodgers this past season, The Athletic reports that scouts and executives "harbored no concerns" about Skubal's medicals.

"From an eye-test perspective, multiple scouts and executives, who spoke anonymously so as to candidly discuss the matter, said they harbored no concerns about Skubal's physical condition or mechanics," Sammon, Rosenthal, and Woo continued. "Even when he missed time with injuries, the evaluators said, Skubal consistently returned relatively quickly to produce elite value. One executive who monitored Skubal closely over the final two months of 2026 said Skubal's 925 1/3 innings since his call-up in 2020 make him as durable as anyone."

At any rate, it remains to be seen which teams break the bank to go after Skubal this offseason, as he projects to get a $400 million contract.

MLB rumors: A new Tarik Skubal suitor, Braves' Ronald Acuńa Jr. plan, plus Blue Jays, Cubs, Red Sox, more

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

This is an important week on the baseball calendar. First and foremost, it is awards week. Kevin McGonigle and Sal Stewart were named the Rookies of the Year on Monday. The Managers of the Year will be announced Tuesday, Cy Youngs and Relievers of the Year will be announced Wednesday, and finally the MVPs will be announced Thursday.

Also, the GM Meetings are this week in Las Vegas. The GM Meetings are not the Winter Meetings in terms of the sheer volume of rumors and transactions, but they do usually provide some news. There could be more than usual this year given the near certainty of a lockout on Dec. 1, which would also mean there are no Winter Meetings. This may be the only time everyone is in the same room this winter. The three weeks between now and Dec. 1 could be quite active.

We've already ranked the top 50 free agents and the top 25 trade candidates.

With that in mind, here are Tuesday's hot stove rumors.

The Blue Jays are among the teams with interest in free agent second baseman Brandon Lowe, according to Sportsnet. Ernie Clement, Toronto's primary second baseman in 2026, crashed hard in the second half and has always fit better as a super utility man who plays regularly at different positions. Josh Smith is the team's other second base option.

Lowe would bring needed power to a team that finished dead last in home runs this past season and had just one player, Kazuma Okamoto (33), clear 15 home runs. Only four Blue Jays hit even 10 homers. Also, Toronto's lineup is mostly right-handed. Lowe would add power and also a lefty bat to provide balance. We ranked him as the 13th-best free agent available.

Adding rotation help is a "top priority," White Sox GM Chris Getz said during an MLB Network Radio interview on Tuesday. "We're going to exhaust the starter market, both free agency -- we'll take a look -- along with trades. That is our top priority," Getz said. He also confirmed that Grant Taylor, the team's postseason closer, will move into the rotation. Hagen Smith will remain in the bullpen.

The White Sox have solid enough rotation depth with Taylor joining Sean Burke, Luis Castillo, Anthony Kay, Davis Martin, David Sandlin, Noah Schultz, and perhaps Shane Smith and Drew Thorpe. There is room for improvement, though, particularly at the top. Chicago could use a bona fide ace, not that one is easy to acquire or abundantly available.

Add the Padres to the list of teams that have shown interest in two-time Cy Young winner Tarik Skubal, reports MLB Network. San Diego has a major need in the rotation -- Joe Musgrove, who hasn't made a start since the 2024 postseason, and Randy Vásquez are their only rotation locks -- and a new ownership group that seems willing to spend in its first offseason.

Skubal is far and away the offseason's best free agent. He is also a Scott Boras client, and, historically, top Boras clients take the largest contract offer regardless of team situation. That isn't to say the Padres are a bad situation. Not at all. They're a very good team that is in the postseason most years, plus they play in San Diego. Who doesn't love San Diego?

The Padres are also connected to several Japanese players, according to The Athletic. That includes MLB free agents Seiya Suzuki and Shota Imanaga, as well as players coming over from Nippon Professional Baseball, like third baseman Teruaki Sato.

Free agent lefty Adrian Morejon is drawing interest as a starting pitcher, but he prefers to remain in the bullpen, according to The Athletic. Even during the season, there were rumblings that Morejon would like to be given a chance to start in 2027. He came up through the minors as a starter and only remained in the bullpen because he was so dominant in a late-inning role.

We ranked Morejon as the offseason's second-best free agent on the idea that he would move into the rotation. The belief within the game is that he has a chance to be an excellent starter, not just a back-end piece. Either way, Morejon is a very good pitcher, and at 28, he's also one of the youngest free agents available. He should have several peak years remaining.

Although they are disinclined to pursue Tarik Skubal, the Cubs hope to add three established starting pitchers this offseason, reports The Athletic. Of the nine pitchers to make at least nine starts for Chicago in 2027, seven are free agents. Only Ben Brown, who recently had neck surgery (per ESPN), and Edward Cabrera, who had a 5.96 ERA and was demoted to the bullpen, remain.

Left-hander Justin Steele is expected back from UCL surgery next year, though he suffered a setback this summer and wasn't able to return as hoped in September. Others like Javier Assad and Jordan Wicks are depth options more than full-time rotation pieces. This free agent class offers plenty of second- and third-tier starters. That's where the Cubs are expected to shop.

Unlike last offseason, the Mets are not interested in a major overhaul despite their disappointing year, reports MLB.com. The Mets went 74-88 and finished in last place in 2026. The only silver lining to the disappointing season was the ability to give a long runway to rookies like Carson Benge and Nolan McLean, both of whom finished in the top four of the Rookie of the Year voting.

The Mets went 83-79 in 2025 and missed the postseason on the final day of the season. They made significant changes last winter, most notably jettisoning stalwarts Pete Alonso and Brandon Nimmo, then had a worse season in 2026. In September, president of baseball operations David Stearns admitted that much turnover created an "uncapped downside scenario," which is executive-speak for "there was too much risk."

Not surprisingly, the Braves are uninterested in moving former National League MVP Ronald Acuña Jr., according to MLB Network. It does feel like Atlanta's offense has gone stale and that they could move a core piece, but it won't be Acuña, nor is it likely to be Drake Baldwin, Michael Harris II, or Matt Olson. Ozzie Albies and Austin Riley are the more obvious players to move. Albies is entering the final season of his wildly team-friendly contract. Riley has six years remaining on his deal, which figures to make a move difficult.

On Monday, Red Sox chief baseball officer Craig Breslow declined to commit to Trevor Story as his starting shortstop next season. "We still have a ton of confidence in Trevor, but any decisions like that are going to take place between Trevor and me far before I speak to anything publicly," Breslow told reporters, including the Boston Globe. Boston has one of the top prospects in the minors in shortstop Franklin Arias, who is very likely to make his debut early next year. Arias could push Story into a utility role or off the team entirely.

Everything We Know About ‘Off Campus’ Season 2 So Far

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Prime Video’s Off Campus received a season 2 renewal before its first season even launched, and the central couple is poised to take the torch from Hannah Wells (Ella Bright) and Garrett Graham (Belmont Cameli).

If anything, Season 2 has a high bar to live up to with singer songwriter Remi Wolf appearing in Season 1 as one example of the loaded soundtrack. In terms of needle drops, there could likely be more showtunes since the second season will likely focus on theatre.

Read on for everything we know about Off Campus Season 2 so far: Watch on Deadline

The second season will return April 28, 2027 to Prime Video under a year after the first one premiered in May. It will be interesting to see if Prime Video opts to release the second season episodically every week like the streamer did with later seasons of The Summer I Turned Pretty or in a whole batch for binging like they did with Season 1.

Who is the central couple of Off Campus Season 2?

Prime Video confirmed two weeks after the first season launched that Mika Abdalla and Stephen Kalyn would be taking center stage in Season 2 as Allie Hayes and Dean Di Laurentis. Their book in Elle Kennedy’s series that inspired the show, The Score, comes third in the order after The Mistake which centers John Logan and Grace Ivers.

Will Hannah and Garrett be back in Season 2 of Off Campus?

Yes, the couple from Elle Kennedy’s first book, The Deal will continue to feature in Season 2, living out their happily ever after.

RELATED: ‘Off Campus’ Stars Ella Bright & Belmont Cameli Talk Lighthearted, Serious And Musical “Moments Of Connection” For Hannah & Garrett In Season 1

“We love the couples, so there is a passing of the baton that happens, but we also don’t want Hannah and Garrett to ride off into the sunset and disappear from the landscape of our show,” showrunner Louisa Levy told Deadline. “They are still very much present in season two, and are a big part of the community of characters that we’re building on the show.”

Will Josh Heuston’s Justin Kohl be back in Off Campus Season 2?

Unfortunately, Josh Heuston won’t be back for season 2 as Justin Kohl, which Levy confirmed to TV Guide.

In Elle Kennedy’s books, Justin ends up romantically involved with a girl named Stella, a friend of Allie’s in The Score. Eagle-eyed viewers spotted a penant with the name Stella emblazoned on it on the door across the hall from Allie and Hannah’s dorm room when Sean (Riley Davis) stops by to try and win her back.

“I love Josh and I would not write him off if I can find a way to bring Justin back at some point. We never got a chance to meet Stella, who is the person he ends up with in the book,” she said. “So maybe we’ll find a way to fold him into a future season. We don’t have him [available] next season, but never say never.”

RELATED: Josh Heuston & Ryan Corr To Star In Greg McLean’s Biblical Horror ‘The First Exorcist’; Upgrade Launches For Cannes Market

Heuston told Deadline that he had fun filming the show, seeming open to a return later down the line.

Will there be new characters in Season 2 of Off Campus?

Yes, before Season 1 launched, Deadline broke the news of two major castings for Season 2 of the series: Philippa Soo as Scarlett, a theatre artist guest directing an original play in Briar U’s drama department, and India Fowler as Grace Ivers — love interest of Antonio Cipriano’s John Logan.

Will there be more seasons of Off Campus?

No official renewal or greenlight have been served yet for a third season, but the idea is that each of the core four Briar U hockey teammates will get their love story in future seasons, with potential for more Briar U book characters to be pulled in as well, like Charlie Evans’ Hunter Davenport, for example. Hunter, who was first introduced as Carter St. James V, proved a major dark horse character in Season 1 and a big twist for Dean and Allie’s love story.

With Grace set to appear in Season 2, it seems Logan’s love story could unfold in Season 3, and she was even name-dropped in Season 1 at the auction at Malone’s.

Levy co-showruns and executive produces Off Campus with Gina Fattore. Wyck Godfrey, Marty Bowen and James Seidman executive produce for Temple Hill. Leanna Billings of Billings Productions and Neal Flaherty also serve as executive producers.

Man Utd Manager Target Simone Inzaghi 'Decides to Leave' Al-Hilal

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

It is looking increasingly likely that Manchester United will be looking to appoint their next manager in the coming hours or days, and one of their targets has just decided to leave his club, according to an Italian journalist.

Man United chiefs gave Michael Carrick a stay of execution following the successive defeats to Como and Bournemouth, although it is clear that they wanted to see an improvement in the next three matches prior to the international break.

Carrick lost the first of those as the Red Devils were beaten 2-0 against Chelsea at Stamford Bridge, and it looked as though it would be curtains for the 45-year-old when his team found themselves 2-0 down at home against AS Roma in the Champions League.

United produced a stunning comeback to win that 3-2, before Carrick found himself in a similar situation against Unai Emery's Aston Villa on Sunday.

His side were 2-0 down after just four minutes, but they twice proved their battling qualities to come from two goals down to draw 4-4, with their final two goals both coming in injury time.

It doesn't appear it was enough to convince Jason Wilcox and Omar Berrada that he should continue in the job after the international break though, with the duo expected to recommend to Sir Jim Ratcliffe in meetings in Monaco this week that he should be relieved of his duties.

Should that happen, then United will be looking for their next manager, and there are plenty of names appreciated by the hierarchy.

GIVEMESPORT sources revealed that Mauricio Pochettino is emerging as a leading contender for the job out of nowhere, although the Red Devils will need to pay $10m to activate the release clause in his contract as the United States manager.

GMS understands that three free-agent managers in Eddie Howe, Julian Nagelsmann and Antonio Conte also have their admirers on the board, as well as Simone Inzaghi.

According to a journalist in Italy, posting on X, the former Inter Milan boss has now decided to leave Saudi Pro League side Al-Hilal when his contract expires at the end of the 2026-27 season.

While the fact that he may not be available until the end of the season could count against him, there is no doubt that INEOS will be on red alert if he has decided to leave his current role in 2027.

Inzaghi will be one of the best free-agent managers available on the market should he leave Al-Hilal, and not just because of what he has achieved in Saudi Arabia.

The 52-year-old has won 52 of his 69 matches in charge of Al-Hilal, having enjoyed similarly fruitful spells in his home country leading Inter Milan and Lazio.

Inzaghi may be from a background of the notoriously defensive Serie A, but we have seen the striker's attacking instincts come through in his teams' playing style instead.

When he left Inter, they had a goals-per-game record that sat just under two, with a goal difference of +237.

Not only did he win one Serie A title during his time in charge at the San Siro, but he also came close to winning the Champions League on two occasions, with the club beaten finalists in 2023 and 2025.

LISA of BLACKPINK to Release New Song ‘Leave With Me’ as ‘Always Lalisa’ Doc Heads to Streaming

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

As previously announced, the film will soon become available on YouTube Premium.

LISA of BLACKPINK‘s documentary, Always Lalisa, and its end-credits song, “Leave With Me,” both finally have a release date on streaming services.

As announced Tuesday (Nov. 10), the K-pop star will release her Sue Kim-directed film — which chronicled LISA’s life as she navigated her first year focusing on solo projects away from her girl group — on YouTube Premium on Nov. 18. That same day, “Leave With Me” will finally drop after fans first heard it during the end credits when Always Lalisa was in theaters.

The movie first premiered at the Toronto International Film Festival in September, shortly after Always Lalisa‘s destination on YouTube Premium was announced. Following the TIFF premiere, it opened in theaters and IMAX in October.

“Forging her own path, particularly in the Western music market, brings new pressures, relentless scrutiny and the challenge of redefining herself on her own terms,” a synopsis of the doc reads. “Intimate, emotional and revealing, Always Lalisa captures an artist at a crossroads, documenting a year of risk, reinvention and self-discovery as LISA begins to define not only her next chapter, but the woman and artist she wants to become.”

The White Lotus alum is now gearing up to play six November shows at The Colosseum at Caesars Palace in Las Vegas for her Viva La Lisa residency. She’s currently fresh off the release of her Press Play EP, which featured lead single “SaWaDiKa” and debuted at No. 35 on the Billboard 200. Her debut solo LP, Alter Ego — which LISA made and released during her time away from BLACKPINK in 2025, as chronicled in her documentary — reached No. 7 on the albums chart.

Batman Just Officially Saved DC Comics From a Terrible Fate

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Reports also indicated DC Comics boss Jim Lee has ‘welcomed’ the news of James Gunn and Peter Safran’s ousting

Business mergers always come with shake-ups, and Skydance's recent acquisition of Warner Bros. Discovery was inevitably going to be messy in some way. What was unexpected, however, was this week's announcement that James Gunn and Peter Safran would be stepping down as co-heads of DC Studios. At the same time, Skydance has initiated layoffs in other divisions such as Skydance Games. One area that seems to have escaped the chopping block for now, though, is DC Comics.

Bleeding Cool reports that, according to multiple sources, no one at DC Comics has been offered voluntary buyouts like other divisions were this October. The surprising reason for this comes down to performance. Apparently, DC Comics is outperforming a number of other Warner Bros. departments and is actively turning a profit. Much of that success is directly laid at the feet of Absolute Batman, Scott Snyder and Nick Dragotta's tent-pole series for the publisher's phenomenal Absolute line. Absolute Batman has been both a critical and commercial success, helping DC surpass Marvel in sales. As Bleeding Cool puts it, "no one wants to kill that golden batgoose." There is also an animated adaptation of the comic currently in the works.

There may be another wrinkle to the story, however. The report also states that the company's organizational structure makes roles and responsibilities unclear, meaning that Skydance does not want to risk accidently firing someone integral to the success of books like Absolute Batman. Due to this nebulous organization, Batman seems to have saved DC Comics from going through a major shake-up.

A shocking part of Bleeding Cool's report also revealed that DC Comics publisher, president, and CCO Jim Lee has "welcomed the news of Jeremy Latcham's appointment." Latcham is replacing Gunn and Safran. According to Bleeding Cool's sources, Lee has felt like he has been frozen out at DC Studios by the outgoing pair. This has instigated Lee's decision to work more publicly on projects outside of DC, perhaps preparing to exit his role. That may change with Gunn and Safran gone.

At the same time, rumors are swirling that DC scribe Tom King may be ousted as well, or perhaps just be looking to jump ship. The writer has penned a number of well-received runs at DC, such as Supergirl: Woman of Tomorrow, Mister Miracle, Omega Men, and more. He was also tapped by Gunn to be an architect of the new DC Universe, with several projects being based on his comic work. King also helped create HBO's Lanterns. Now, it seems like King could be leaving DC for a possible return to Marvel. With his divisive Wonder Woman run about to end and Gunn and Safran out, it might be the best opportunity for King to go on to other things.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

More fuel for the fire of those of us assuming the Ellisons are going to focus almost entirely on Batman going forward.
